### 1.1.1 ìÞÿí ûñÇ ì¼Ý Í¾ý ¿ ÚÖÎï ¿ çÚï ½

## 2.3 Í¾ý ýùü ý¬ìïÿ Îïºû ¼¼

### 2.3.1 ºû ¼¼Ç ÇÝ Ý÷³Á Í¾ý Öÿ

2.2ÈÕÝ¶ É½ÁÚ÷ öÇþïÖ ¿ Ö³ÖìÅß 11¼ ÉíÕ ³Ý 330¼ Í¾ýß Ö®½üï Ú÷¬î ó÷ñÆ æùúûñ ìïÿ ýìß þü ìð¼ æùúûñ ìïùÇ ÷ïÝí ºû Ý÷Õ ¬ïÝ Í¾ýÇ áüÝÆö.

### BIBLIOGRAPHIC DATA SHEET

<table>
<tr><th>(1) Classification of Technology</th><th colspan="3">(2) Research Method</th><th>(3) Submission Date Dec. 31, 2024</th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3">(4) Title/Subtitle Advanced water distribution management based on comprehensive data analysis of scattered pilot smart meter</td><td colspan="2">(5) Research Period Jan. 1, 2023 ~ Dec. 31, 2024</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3">(6) Performing Organization K-water Research Institute</td><td colspan="2">(7) Author(s) Kyoung Pil Kim</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3">(8) Performing Organization Address 125 Yuseong-daero 1689beon-gil, Yuseong-gu, Daejeon</td><td colspan="2">(9) Sponsoring Agency</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="5">(10) Abstracts Smart meter data is invaluable not only for solving and improving various issues faced by water supply systems, such as water demand forecasting, leakage management, water quality management, energy management, and customer service enhancement, but also for its immense value from a socio-cultural perspective. In Korea, smart meters have been introduced through several national projects, but the adoption is still at a pilot stage. Transitioning to smart meter-based operation and management is expected to require significant time and costs. This study aimed to develop water loss management technologies that can be utilized in DMAs without smart meters by collecting scattered smart meter data in pilot stages. Using the Statistical Geographic Information Service (SGIS) data, we divided 330 DMAs in local governments into four groups, and then extracted DMAs with a smart meter water usage measurement ratio of 50%, and developed a model for predicting the average night water consumption during the minimum night flow. This model was expanded to monitor time-series minimum night flow using daily and hourly water usage patterns extracted from DMAs with similar socio-demographic characteristics. This expansion supports determining the timing of leakage inspections in DMAs without smart meters. Additionally, using smart water care data that measures water consumption at individual faucets, piloted in Busan EDC (Eco Delta City), we analyzed seasonal and daily variations in water usage, performed water usage pattern and component analyses considering resident population characteristics, and proposed practical applications for these findings.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="5">(11) Keyword water distribution system, smart water meter, minimum night flow, leakage, SGIS</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="5">(12) # Tag G230219</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2">(13) Security Yes</td><td colspan="3">(14) No. of Pages<br>142</td></tr>
</table>
